From the testimonies, Kay mentions seeing someone walking towards her from the other side of the candy stand (Testimony 4). This implies that Kay was on one side of the candy stand and the mysterious figure was on the opposite side. 

In Evidence 8, the Bigg Building Pamphlet describes the layout of the viewing platform, including the location of the candy stand. According to the pamphlet, the candy stand is located on the right-most edge of the viewing platform.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us identify a contradiction based on the spatial information provided.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states: "If Kay is in front of the candy stand and the mysterious figure is behind the candy stand then the candy stand is between Kay and the mysterious figure."

This proposition applies because it directly relates to the spatial positioning of Kay and the mysterious figure relative to the candy stand. If Kay saw the mysterious figure walking towards her from the other side of the candy stand, it means the candy stand was between them, which aligns with Proposition 1.

However, the contradiction arises when we consider the spatial positioning described in Testimony 4 and the layout given in Evidence 7. Kay's recollection suggests she was pushed from beyond the candy stand, implying she was closer to the edge where the candy stand is located. Yet, the autopsy report (Evidence 11) indicates that the stab wounds were inflicted post-mortem, meaning Kay would not have been conscious to observe the direction from which the push came.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

While Proposition 1 does apply, the contradiction is more about the timing and consciousness of Kay rather than the spatial positioning alone. The autopsy report (Evidence 11) contradicts Kay's testimony (Testimony 4) because if Kay was unconscious due to the fatal head injury, she could not have seen the mysterious figure walking towards her from the other side of the candy stand.

This suggests that Phoenix Wright might have held the bottle with his left hand, which contradicts the implication that he grabbed the bottle with his right hand as suggested by Olga's testimony.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 1 applies and reveals the contradiction. The spatial reasoning is based on the orientation of the fingerprints on the bottle, which suggests a different hand usage than implied by the testimony.

In this case, Proposition 4 does apply and helps us identify the contradiction. From the testimonies, Atishon states that Dr. Buff was standing in front of the bookshelves and was struck from behind by Datz Are'bal using a suitcase. This implies that the suitcase was swung from behind Dr. Buff towards his head.

However, Evidence 3 (Study Photo) indicates that the victim is lying in the study next to floor-to-ceiling windows with clear glass and is covered by books. This suggests that the victim fell forward onto the books, which contradicts the idea of being struck from behind and falling backward.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

Proposition 3 states: "If Dr. Buff is hit from the front with the suitcase then Dr. Buff falls backward."

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 3 does not directly apply because it talks about being hit from the front, whereas Atishon claims Datz Are'bal struck Dr. Buff from behind. However, we can infer that if Dr. Buff was hit from behind, he would likely fall forward, not backward.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial reasoning used here is based on the direction of force applied to Dr. Buff. If he was struck from behind, the force would push him forward, causing him to fall face down. But the Study Photo shows him covered by books, suggesting he fell forward, which contradicts Atishon's claim that Datz Are'bal struck him from behind.
